    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n17\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.2, F32.1, F43.2, F41.2\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, it’s good to meet you. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I guess I’m here because I’ve just stopped caring about things. Stuff I used to look forward to doesn’t do anything for me anymore. My fishing gear’s just sitting in the garage untouched, my woodshop feels like another chore, and even when my kids try to get me to play chess, I’m just… kind of going through the motions.\n\nDoctor: Did this loss of interest begin fairly suddenly, or has it been there most of the time for years?\n\nPatient: It wasn’t really sudden. It’s been there most of the time for a while now—about a year and seven months, I guess. Since my wife’s health problems started changing everything at home.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel this change in you is mainly because of what’s been happening with your wife’s health?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, it lines up with all of that happening, so maybe it’s part of it. But I can’t say it’s only because of her health. It just feels like I changed, and I don’t fully know why.\n\nDoctor: Has this low mood and loss of interest been present nearly every day for a full two years?\n\nPatient: Not a full two years, no. It’s been most days for about a year and seven months, since my wife’s health problems started changing things at home.\n\nDoctor: Has this been making it hard for you to function in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’s been affecting things a lot. At work I’ve started missing things I normally wouldn’t, and in construction that can mess with deadlines, or even safety. There was a near-miss, and after that I couldn’t really tell myself it was just stress anymore.\n\nAt home, I’m there, but not really dependable the way I used to be. My wife and kids have had to work around me instead of being able to count on me.\n\nDoctor: Before this started about a year and seven months ago, had you ever had another period in your life when you felt depressed or lost interest in things for weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve had rough stretches before like anyone, but not where I felt depressed or lost interest in things for weeks at a time like this.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that you’d be better off dead, or thoughts of harming yourself?\n\nPatient: Yes… I have. Mostly on the early drives to work, before anyone else is awake. I catch myself thinking it would be easier if I just didn’t make it there. And sometimes the thought of steering off the road comes up, and it scares me because it feels too close.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed most days?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, I think so. It’s not always the exact same all day, but it’s there a lot. I wake up already feeling low, and it’s been hard to shake.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a big change in your appetite?\n\nPatient: Yeah. Most days I just don’t want food. It kind of tastes like cardboard, so I’ll skip breakfast, and even after being out on a job site all day I’ll just pick at dinner. My belt’s had to come in a couple notches, and I haven’t been trying to lose weight.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel your depression is mainly a reaction to your wife’s health problems?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s when it really started. It’s been going on about a year and seven months now, since my wife’s health problems changed everything at home.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: Yeah. It’s gotten pretty bad. I used to be able to keep schedules and subcontractors straight in my head, but now I’ll read the same email three times and still not really take it in. In meetings I’ll lose my train of thought, forget what I was about to say… and decisions I used to make in five minutes, I just freeze on them.\n\nDoctor: Have other people noticed that you’ve been moving or speaking more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t had anyone say that to me directly. I feel slowed down inside, but as far as people noticing my speech or how I move… I don’t know.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel noticeably slowed down in your movements?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I feel worn down and kind of heavy, but I can’t say I’ve noticed my movements being obviously slower.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I’m exhausted when I get into bed, but I’ll just lie there thinking about bills and schedules. Or I wake up around three or four in the morning and then I’m just staring at the ceiling until it’s time to head to the site.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless?\n\nPatient: Yeah. A lot. I keep thinking I’ve let everybody down—my wife, my kids, the guys at work. If anything goes wrong, even a missed appointment or a cost overrun, I take it like proof that I’m just useless… like everyone would be better off if I wasn’t the one in charge.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling tired or like you have no energy?\n\nPatient: Yeah. By midmorning I already feel like I’ve put in a full day. Even just walking the project or taking the stairs with the crew feels like I’m hauling around wet concrete. I’m just drained.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge a lot?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, I do feel wound up sometimes, but I haven’t really paid attention to whether it’s anxiety exactly. It’s more like my mind won’t settle.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

I mean, I do feel wound up sometimes, but I haven’t really paid attention to whether it’s anxiety exactly. It’s more like my mind won’t settle.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you been worrying a lot?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 45 / construction_manager / bachelors\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Plain patient.\nA plain patient describes their symptoms and experiences in a direct and straightforward manner, providing clear and relevant answers to the doctor's questions.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. It’s been there most of the time for a while now—about a year and seven months, I guess. Since my wife’s health problems started changing everything at home.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel this change in you is mainly because of what’s been happening with your wife’s health?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, it lines up with all of that happening, so maybe it’s part of it. But I can’t say it’s only because of her health. It just feels like I changed, and I don’t fully know why.\n\nDoctor: Has this low mood and loss of interest been present nearly every day for a full two years?\n\nPatient: Not a full two years, no. It’s been most days for about a year and seven months, since my wife’s health problems started changing things at home.\n\nDoctor: Has this been making it hard for you to function in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’s been affecting things a lot. At work I’ve started missing things I normally wouldn’t, and in construction that can mess with deadlines, or even safety. There was a near-miss, and after that I couldn’t really tell myself it was just stress anymore.\n\nAt home, I’m there, but not really dependable the way I used to be. My wife and kids have had to work around me instead of being able to count on me.\n\nDoctor: Before this started about a year and seven months ago, had you ever had another period in your life when you felt depressed or lost interest in things for weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve had rough stretches before like anyone, but not where I felt depressed or lost interest in things for weeks at a time like this.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that you’d be better off dead, or thoughts of harming yourself?\n\nPatient: Yes… I have. Mostly on the early drives to work, before anyone else is awake. I catch myself thinking it would be easier if I just didn’t make it there. And sometimes the thought of steering off the road comes up, and it scares me because it feels too close.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed most days?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, I think so. It’s not always the exact same all day, but it’s there a lot. I wake up already feeling low, and it’s been hard to shake.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a big change in your appetite?\n\nPatient: Yeah. My belt’s had to come in a couple notches, and I haven’t been trying to lose weight.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel your depression is mainly a reaction to your wife’s health problems?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s when it really started. I haven’t had anyone say that to me directly. By midmorning I already feel like I’ve put in a full day. Even just walking the project or taking the stairs with the crew feels like I’m hauling around wet concrete. I’m just drained.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge a lot?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, I do feel wound up sometimes, but I haven’t really paid attention to whether it’s anxiety exactly. It’s more like my mind won’t settle.\n\nDoctor: Have you been worrying a lot?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. I’ve got things on my mind, sure, but I’m not sure I’d call it worrying a lot. It’s more just… everything feels hard to shut off.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I’m exhausted when I get into bed, but I’ll just lie there thinking about bills and schedules. Or I wake up around three or four in the morning and then I’m just staring at the ceiling until it’s time to head to the site.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless?\n\nPatient: Yeah. A lot. I keep thinking I’ve let everybody down—my wife, my kids, the guys at work. If anything goes wrong, even a missed appointment or a cost overrun, I take it like proof that I’m just useless… like everyone would be better off if I wasn’t the one in charge.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling tired or like you have no energy?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I’m just drained.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge a lot?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. It’s more like my mind won’t settle.\n\nDoctor: Have you been worrying a lot?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. I’ve got things on my mind, sure, but I’m not sure I’d call it worrying a lot.
